#### Useful notes for your reviewer
Updates KEP-7990. PriorityBoost stays Alpha in v0.21, and Beta is planned for v0.22.
- v0.21 (Alpha): the boost moves from the kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost annotation to a new spec.priorityBoost field on the Workload. Kueue stops reading the annotation.
- v0.22 (Beta target): adds the Beta criteria, the test plan, and the interactions with elastic jobs, Concurrent Admission, MultiKueue and Configurable Preemptions.

AI assistance: drafted with Claude.

## AI summary

This PR updates the KEP documents for Support Workload Priority Boost By External Controllers (KEP-7990). It changes two files and no code. The diff adds 387 lines and removes 276.

- `keps/7990-priority-boost/kep.yaml` (+9/-3):
  - Adds `@sohankunkerkar` as an author.
  - Removes `@sohankunkerkar` from the reviewers list.
  - Adds five `see-also` entries: `13396-configurable-preemptions`, `77-dynamically-sized-jobs`, `693-multikueue`, `4136-admission-fair-sharing`, and `8691-concurrent-admission`.
  - Changes `stage` from `alpha` to `beta`.
  - Changes `latest-milestone` from `v0.17` to `v0.21`.
  - Adds `beta: "v0.21"` under `milestone`. The alpha milestone remains `v0.17`.
- `keps/7990-priority-boost/README.md` (+378/-273): Rewrites the KEP for the Beta design of the `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` Workload annotation. The changes cover:
  - Effective priority, which is base priority plus the signed boost, used for ordering and preemption. Configurable Preemptions can select base priority instead.
  - Validation limits and feature-gate behavior.
  - Re-evaluation after boost changes.
  - Derived Workload handling. Later boost changes are not copied to derived Workloads.
  - Interactions with other features.
  - The experimental time-slicing reference controller.
  - Test plan, graduation criteria, risks, and alternatives.

The PR description says that limiting a boost to one ClusterQueue depends on Configurable Preemptions, which is Alpha. The author asks the reviewer whether this dependency is acceptable for Beta. The diff does not resolve this question.

## Suggested release note

This PR changes KEP documentation only. It has no user-facing code change, so the release note is `NONE`.

```text
NONE
```

**Explanation**

The title names KEP-7990 and two changes: keeping PriorityBoost in Alpha for v0.21 and moving the boost to a Workload field. The raw summary does not show a move of the boost to a Workload field. It describes a rewritten README and kep.yaml changes that advance the target stage from alpha to beta and add a beta milestone at v0.21. The objectives describe a Beta graduation update. The title therefore conflicts with the changeset on the stage and on the field move, so the title is misleading.

**Resolution**

Rewrite the title to match the changeset. The kep.yaml change advances the stage to beta, so a title such as 'KEP-7990: Update PriorityBoost for Beta graduation' fits the evidence. If the boost truly moves to a Workload field, confirm that change exists in the diff and mention it only if the README shows it. Also confirm whether the stage stays Alpha or moves to Beta.

### Comment by [@mimowo](https://github.com/mimowo) — 2026-11-19T11:03:21Z

> @sohankunkerkar As I mentioned previously, I would consider having dedicated field in Workload object. In the alpha phase, we didn't do that since we don't want to unstable field to Beta Resource. But, if we graduate it to beta, we should do that.

I'm not sure we need the field actually. If we need, then we probably would need to have some kind of owner reference, as orchestratorRef in DQO, which is bigger effort.

 I think we could still go to beta with annot and deprecate / delete the annotation later when the field is proven to be needed. This is related to the deprecation policy, I think Beta APIs should be breakable with prior notice. I will be preparing a new deprecation policy to clarify that,

I'm ok either way, but if we go with field I think it is too late for v0.21. We need to first incubate the fielf for one release in alpha I think

### Comment by [@sohankunkerkar](https://github.com/sohankunkerkar) — 2026-11-19T14:58:25Z

> > @sohankunkerkar As I mentioned previously, I would consider having dedicated field in Workload object. In the alpha phase, we didn't do that since we don't want to unstable field to Beta Resource. But, if we graduate it to beta, we should do that.
> 
> I'm not sure we need the field actually. If we need, then we probably would need to have some kind of owner reference, as orchestratorRef in DQO, which is bigger effort.
> 
> I think we could still go to beta with annot and deprecate / delete the annotation later when the field is proven to be needed. This is related to the deprecation policy, I think Beta APIs should be breakable with prior notice. I will be preparing a new deprecation policy to clarify that,
> 
> I'm ok either way, but if we go with field I think it is too late for v0.21. We need to first incubate the fielf for one release in alpha I think

I think the field can still work for v0.21. The one-release incubation is there so an older API server in the same cluster doesn't drop a new field, and a CRD has one schema per cluster. What's left is downgrade and an older MultiKueue worker, and I've added both to the KEP. The semantics are unchanged since v0.17, only the carrier moves. I prototyped the field for preemption, elastic and MultiKueue sync, and the change is small.

On the owner reference: the booster already overwrites a boost someone else sets on a Workload it manages, so that conflict exists with the annotation too. I'd track it separately from field vs annotation. WDYT?

### Comment by [@mimowo](https://github.com/mimowo) — 2026-11-19T15:21:28Z

> I think the field can still work for v0.21. The one-release incubation is there so an older API server in the same cluster doesn't drop a new field, and a CRD has one schema per cluster. What's left is downgrade and an older MultiKueue worker, and I've added both to the KEP. The semantics are unchanged since v0.17, only the carrier moves. I prototyped the field for preemption, elastic and MultiKueue sync, and the change is small.

Maybe, but we release next week, we have still 5+ KEPs to merge + impls, so starting a new KEP in beta phase is risky. Certainly beta API has higher quality bar than alpha. Thus, I'm proposing to try alpha for 0.21, but then fix bugs before going to beta.

Sounds good, I'll update the KEP: `spec.priorityBoost` lands as Alpha in v0.21 and Beta moves to v0.22 with the bug fixes as criteria.

 ## Summary
 
-Introduce an optional `Workload` annotation `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost`
-that allows external controllers to boost (or reduce) a workload's effective
-priority. Kueue computes the **effective priority** as:
+This KEP adds an optional `Workload` annotation, `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost`,
+so that an external controller can raise or lower the priority that Kueue uses for a Workload.
+Kueue adds the boost to the Workload's priority and uses the result wherever it compares Workload priorities.
+The Pod priority does not change.
 
-`effectivePriority = workload.priority + priorityBoost`
-
-and uses it wherever workload priority is currently used within a ClusterQueue,
-including scheduling order, preemption candidate ordering, and preemption
-eligibility evaluation.
+## Motivation
 
-This enables platform teams to dynamically adjust workload priority based on
-runtime signals (e.g. checkpoint status, execution phase, number of prior
-preemptions), without hardcoding cost policies into Kueue and without requiring
-a proliferation of WorkloadPriority classes.
+The cost of preempting a Workload is not uniform.
+Some Workloads are cheap to interrupt, for example while they initialize.
+Others are expensive, for example in the middle of an epoch with no checkpoint.
+Currently Kueue decides preemption from the ClusterQueue, the fairness configuration, the Workload priority and recency.
+Without the boost, it cannot use a runtime signal from an external system.
 
-## Motivation
+Currently the priority of a Workload changes only through its priority class.
+Platform teams need to adjust it while the Workload runs:
 
-The "cost" of preempting a workload is not uniform. Some workloads are cheap to
-interrupt (e.g. still initializing), while others are expensive (e.g. mid-epoch
-without checkpoint). Kueue today makes preemption decisions primarily based on
-cluster queue, fairness configuration, workload priority, and recency; it has no
-mechanism to incorporate runtime cost signals from external systems.
-
-Existing priority classes are static: a workload receives a priority at creation
-time and keeps it for its lifetime. In practice, platform teams need a way to
-adjust effective priority dynamically:
-
-- **Visibility**: an explicit annotation makes it clear to both users and
-  external controllers that the priority was boosted, avoiding repeated or
-  conflicting adjustments.
-- **Fine-grained priority without class proliferation**: with a numeric boost,
-  controllers can express priorities like "150" or "156" without creating a zoo
-  of WorkloadPriority classes for every combination of base priority and runtime
-  state.
-- **Controller convenience**: the annotation lives on the `Workload` object, so
-  the external controller only needs to operate on a single Kind rather than
-  every schedulable object type (Job, JobSet, Deployment, etc.).
-
-An important use case is preventing starvation of low-priority workloads: if a
-low-priority workload has been preempted too many times and has never completed,
-an external controller can boost it above medium-priority workloads so it
-eventually makes progress.
+- **Visibility**: an annotation shows users and controllers that the priority was adjusted, and by how much.
+- **Fine-grained priority without many classes**: a numeric boost can express a priority such as 150 or 156
+  without a WorkloadPriorityClass for every combination of base priority and runtime state.
+- **One object kind**: the annotation is on the `Workload`, so the external controller handles one kind,
+  not every job kind.
+- **A supported way to change priority at runtime**: some users edit `spec.priority` by hand
+  ([#14698](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/14698)).
+  Kueue can overwrite such an edit, for example when kueue-controller-manager restarts.
 
 ### Goals
 
-- Add a new optional `Workload` annotation (`kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost`)
-  which allows external controllers to boost priority for some workloads.
-- Define effective priority as `workload.priority + priorityBoost` and use it
-  for workload ordering within a ClusterQueue (scheduling and preemption).
-- Provide a reference external controller that demonstrates how to compute and
-  set `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` on `Workload`.
+- Add an optional `Workload` annotation that lets an external controller adjust the priority Kueue uses.
+- Use the adjusted priority in every place where Kueue compares Workload priorities.
+- Provide a reference external controller that sets the annotation.
 
 ### Non-Goals
 
-- Define a universal "correct" cost or boost model inside Kueue.
-- Impact DRS (Dominant Resource Share) calculations; the boost only affects
-  priority-based ordering within a ClusterQueue.
-- Define anti-flapping policies inside Kueue; it is the responsibility of the
-  external controller to avoid pathological update patterns.
-- Provide guarantees about graceful termination or checkpointing.
-- Expose Job-level APIs (annotations) for priority boost; the mechanism stays
-  Workload-only so that batch users cannot set boost on their Jobs.
+- Define a cost or boost model inside Kueue.
+- Change how Kueue computes the Dominant Resource Share (DRS).
+- Limit the effect of a boost to one ClusterQueue.
+  [Configurable Preemptions](../13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md) can scope the effect on preemption.
+- Prevent repeated preemption between Workloads inside Kueue.
+  The external controller must avoid it.
+- Guarantee graceful termination or checkpointing.
+- Let batch users set the boost on their Jobs.
+  The annotation is honored on the `Workload` only.
 
 ## Proposal
 
-Add an optional `Workload` annotation `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` that
-external controllers can set to dynamically adjust a workload's effective
-priority. Kueue incorporates this signal into both scheduling order and
-preemption candidate ordering within a ClusterQueue.
+An external controller writes one signed integer to the `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` annotation of a `Workload`.
+Kueue adds it to the Workload's priority.
+Kueue uses the sum for queue order, for preemption eligibility and for the order of preemption candidates.
+Kueue never computes or changes the annotation.
+
+### Terminology
+
+- **Base priority**: the value of the Workload's `spec.priority`.
+- **Boost**: the integer in the `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` annotation. It is 0 when the annotation is absent.
+- **Effective priority**: base priority plus boost.
+- **External controller**: a controller outside kueue-controller-manager that writes the annotation.
+- **Derived Workload**: a Workload that Kueue creates from another Workload, or for the same job as another Workload:
+  a replacement slice of an elastic job, a variant under Concurrent Admission, or a remote copy under MultiKueue.
 
 ### User Stories
 
-- As a cluster administrator, I want Kueue to preferentially preempt workloads
-  that are cheaper to interrupt, even when multiple candidates share the same
-  WorkloadPriority class.
-- As a platform team, I want to implement organization-specific logic (e.g.
-  checkpoint-aware, phase-aware, starvation-prevention) that dynamically adjusts
-  workload priority and have Kueue respect it without modifying the scheduler.
-- As a platform team, I want to boost a low-priority workload that has been
-  preempted too many times so that it can cross priority class boundaries and
-  eventually make progress.
+#### Story 1: equal-priority Workloads wait behind a long-running Workload
 
-### Effective priority
+Two Workloads have the same priority and each needs the whole quota of a ClusterQueue.
+The second one waits until the first one finishes.
+The platform team wants them to take turns.
 
-Wherever Kueue currently uses workload priority for ordering within a
-ClusterQueue, it uses instead:
+An external controller lowers the effective priority of the running Workload after it has run for one hour:
 
-`effectivePriority = workload.priority + priorityBoost`
+```yaml
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Workload
+metadata:
+  name: train-a
+  annotations:
+    kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost: "-100"
+```
 
-where `priorityBoost` is read from the `Workload` annotation
-`kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` (default `0`).
+The waiting Workload now has a higher effective priority and preempts the running one.
+This story needs a ClusterQueue with `preemption.withinClusterQueue: LowerPriority`, which is not the default.
```

-Add an optional `Workload` annotation `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` that
-external controllers can set to dynamically adjust a workload's effective
-priority. Kueue incorporates this signal into both scheduling order and
-preemption candidate ordering within a ClusterQueue.

-- As a cluster administrator, I want Kueue to preferentially preempt workloads
-  that are cheaper to interrupt, even when multiple candidates share the same
-  WorkloadPriority class.

-  checkpoint-aware, phase-aware, starvation-prevention) that dynamically adjusts
-  workload priority and have Kueue respect it without modifying the scheduler.

-  preempted too many times so that it can cross priority class boundaries and

+#### Story 1: equal-priority Workloads wait behind a long-running Workload
 
-### Effective priority
+Two Workloads have the same priority and each needs the whole quota of a ClusterQueue.
+The second one waits until the first one finishes.
+The platform team wants them to take turns.
 
-Wherever Kueue currently uses workload priority for ordering within a
-ClusterQueue, it uses instead:
+An external controller lowers the effective priority of the running Workload after it has run for one hour:
 
-`effectivePriority = workload.priority + priorityBoost`
+```yaml
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Workload
+metadata:
+  name: train-a
+  annotations:
+    kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost: "-100"
+```
 
-where `priorityBoost` is read from the `Workload` annotation
-`kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` (default `0`).
+The waiting Workload now has a higher effective priority and preempts the running one.
+This story needs a ClusterQueue with `preemption.withinClusterQueue: LowerPriority`, which is not the default.
 
-The boost can cross priority class boundaries. For example, if priorities are
-`low=100`, `mid=200`, `high=300`, a low-priority workload with
-`priority-boost: "150"` would have effective priority 250, placing it between
-mid and high.
+#### Story 2: a low-priority Workload is preempted again and again
 
-### Defaulting and backward compatibility
+The priorities are low=100, mid=200 and high=300.
+A low-priority Workload was preempted several times by mid-priority Workloads and never finished.
+An external controller raises its effective priority to 250:
 
-- If a workload has no `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` annotation set, it is
-  treated as **zero** boost.
-  - This preserves existing behavior when the annotation is unused.
-- The feature is "opt-in by signal": behavior changes only when an external
-  controller (or administrator) sets the annotation.
+```yaml
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Workload
+metadata:
+  name: batch-low
+  annotations:
+    kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost: "150"
+```
+
+Mid-priority Workloads can no longer preempt it, and it is ordered ahead of them in the queue.
+This story needs a preemption policy that compares priorities, for example `preemption.withinClusterQueue: LowerPriority`,
+which is not the default.
+High-priority Workloads still can.
+
+#### Story 3: a platform team changes the priority of a running Workload
+
+A platform team wants to lower the priority of one running Workload without changing its priority class.
+Currently they edit `spec.priority`, which Kueue does not keep.
+With this KEP they set the annotation, as in Story 1, and the base priority stays as the job defines it.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+#### The boost belongs to one Workload object in one cluster
+
+Kueue does not copy a later change of the boost to derived Workloads.
+[Derived Workloads](#derived-workloads) says what each kind of derived Workload gets.
 
 ### Risks and Mitigations
 
-- **Risk**: Infinite preemption cycles. Introducing a dynamic signal that
-  changes ordering creates the possibility of workloads repeatedly preempting
-  each other (A preempts B, B's boost increases, B preempts A, ...).
-  - **Mitigation**: Kueue uses the boost value as a point-in-time snapshot
-    during a scheduling cycle. The responsibility for avoiding flapping lies
-    with the external controller. The reference controller (see below) is
-    designed to limit oscillation by using bounded, threshold-based increments
-    rather than reacting to every preemption.
-  - For Alpha, Kueue does not implement admission cooldown. This will be
-    evaluated for Beta based on real-world feedback.
-
-- **Risk**: Malicious or misconfigured users set extreme boost values on their
-  workloads to avoid preemption.
-  - **Mitigation**: The annotation is on the `Workload` object, which is not
-    directly user-editable in typical deployments (users submit Jobs; Kueue
-    creates Workloads). We do not plan to expose Job-level annotations for
-    priority boost at beta or GA, to avoid giving batch users direct control of
-    boost. In typical setups, `Workload` is used internally by Kueue and
-    cluster admins; giving batch users writable access to `Workload` is not
-    recommended. Security in practice relies on: (a) restricting who can patch
-    `Workload` (RBAC so only trusted controllers can patch it) and optionally
-    admin-configured validation (e.g. ValidationAdmissionPolicies), and (b)
-    FairSharing can help mitigate excessive priority bumping across teams.
-
-- **Risk**: Users/platform automation set unexpected values, causing undesirable
-  ordering.
-  - **Mitigation**: Document semantics clearly; recommend bounded ranges;
-    preserve deterministic ordering.
-
-- **Risk**: Annotation value is invalid or out of expected range.
-  - **Mitigation**: Reject Workload creation or update when the value is
-    invalid or unparsable.
+#### A boost meant for one ClusterQueue changes preemption in the whole cohort
 
-## Design Details
+Kueue uses the effective priority in every priority comparison, including comparisons between ClusterQueues in a cohort.
+An external controller that lowers the boost for time sharing inside a ClusterQueue also exposes that Workload
+to reclaim and to borrowing preemption from other ClusterQueues.
+With `borrowWithinCohort.maxPriorityThreshold`, a negative boost can move a Workload under the threshold.
+A positive boost protects a Workload from other ClusterQueues in the same way.
+
+Mitigation: the docs state the cohort-wide effect.
+An admin who wants the boost to count only inside a ClusterQueue sets the `preemption` policies of the ClusterQueue
+to `Never` and adds two `PreemptionConfig` rules: `Boosted` for `WithinClusterQueue` and `Base` for `WithinParentCohort`
+([KEP-13396](../13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md)).
+In Alpha the `Base` rule also matches Workloads in the same ClusterQueue.
```

  **Resolve the same-ClusterQueue base-priority overlap before Beta, or keep the scoping in Alpha.**

@@ -199,177 +217,237 @@ metadata:

   namespace: default

-    # Positive values increase effective priority. Unset means 0.

+- The value is a signed integer. A positive value raises the effective priority and a negative value lowers it.
+- An absent annotation means a boost of 0.
+- The boost applies to every source of the base priority: a WorkloadPriorityClass, a Kubernetes PriorityClass,
+  or no class.
+- The annotation is mutable at any time, also on an admitted Workload.
+- Kueue does not copy the annotation from a Job or a Pod to the Workload.
+
 ### Validation
 
-When the `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` annotation is set or updated on a
-Workload, Kueue validates that the value is a valid signed integer. Invalid or
-unparsable values cause the Workload creation or update to be rejected (see API
-changes above).
+Kueue rejects a Workload create or update when:
+
+- the value is not a base-10 signed integer that fits in 32 bits
+- the annotation is present with an empty value.
+
+Currently Kueue validates the value on every update of the Workload.
+A Workload that stored an invalid value while the feature gate was off then rejects every later update,
+including status updates, so Kueue can neither admit it nor finish its deletion.
+From Beta, Kueue validates the value on create and on an update that changes the value.
+
+Kueue does not validate the annotation when the `PriorityBoost` feature gate is off.
+
+### Handling
+
+- For an accepted value, Kueue uses the effective priority in the comparisons listed in
+  [Interaction with other features](#interaction-with-other-features).
+- When Kueue preempts a Workload, it records an event on the preempted Workload and on the preemptor.
+  Both events give the base priority, the boost and the effective priority of the two Workloads.
+- The visibility API reports the base priority of a pending Workload.
+  The position it reports follows the effective priority.
+- When the `PriorityBoost` feature gate is off, Kueue ignores the annotation and uses the base priority.
+  The annotation stays on the Workload and applies again when an admin turns the gate on.
+- If a stored value cannot be parsed, Kueue uses the base priority.
+
+### Re-evaluation after a boost change
+
+A boost change on a pending Workload makes Kueue evaluate that Workload again.
+A boost change on an admitted Workload makes Kueue evaluate the inadmissible Workloads of its ClusterQueue again.
+When the ClusterQueue is in a cohort, Kueue evaluates the inadmissible Workloads of the whole cohort tree,
+because one of them may now be able to preempt it.
+
+### Derived Workloads
+
+| Derived Workload | Boost at creation | Later change of the boost on the original |
+|---|---|---|
+| Replacement slice of an elastic job | Currently not copied. From Beta, Kueue copies it from the slice it replaces. | Does not apply. The replaced slice is finished. |
+| Variant under Concurrent Admission | Copied from the parent Workload. | Not copied. |
+| Remote copy under MultiKueue | Copied from the manager's Workload. | Not copied. |
+
+Kueue creates the replacement slice in the same cluster to continue the same job,
+and an external controller cannot set the annotation before the new slice is evaluated.
+So Kueue keeps the boost across that replacement.
+
+A variant and a remote copy are separate Workloads that can be annotated directly.
+An external controller that wants to boost a job under Concurrent Admission annotates the variants.
+Under MultiKueue the worker cluster decides preemption, so an external controller runs in each worker cluster.
+
+### Interaction with other features
+
+| Feature | What the boost changes |
+|---|---|
+| Queue order in a ClusterQueue | Workloads are ordered by effective priority, then by timestamp. |
+| Admission Fair Sharing | LocalQueue usage is compared before priority. The boost only orders Workloads whose LocalQueues have equal usage. |
+| Order of a scheduling cycle across a cohort | After Workloads that fit without borrowing, a higher effective priority goes first. This needs the `PrioritySortingWithinCohort` feature gate, which is on by default. |
+| Fair Sharing | DRS values do not change. When two ClusterQueues tie on DRS, the Workload with the higher effective priority goes first, under the same feature gate. |
+| `withinClusterQueue` and `reclaimWithinCohort` policies | `LowerPriority` compares effective priorities, and so does `LowerOrNewerEqualPriority` of `withinClusterQueue`. Two Workloads with different base priorities and the same effective priority count as equal. |
+| `borrowWithinCohort.maxPriorityThreshold` | The threshold is compared with the effective priority of the candidate. |
+| Order of preemption candidates | Candidates with a lower effective priority are preempted first, after the existing rules for evicted Workloads, other ClusterQueues and LocalQueue usage. |
+| Configurable Preemptions (Alpha) | A priority selector chooses `Boosted` (effective priority) or `Base` (base priority). In Alpha, configured candidates are added after the classical ones, and the classical rules use the effective priority. |
+| Scheduling equivalence classes | The effective priority is part of the class. Two Workloads that differ only in boost are in different classes. |
+| WorkloadPriorityClass value change | Kueue updates the base priority. The boost stays, so the effective priority moves by the same amount. |
+| Elastic jobs, Concurrent Admission, MultiKueue | See [Derived Workloads](#derived-workloads). |
+| Topology Aware Scheduling | No change. |
 
-### Scheduler changes
+### Reference controller
 
-Kueue-scheduler uses **effective priority** for all mechanics where **base
-priority** is currently used. In particular, it is used for:
+`kueue-priority-booster` is an experimental controller in the Kueue repository.
+Admins deploy it separately from kueue-controller-manager.
+It implements Story 1:
 
-1. Ordering of heads for scheduling.
-2. Selection of preemption candidates.
+1. While a Workload is admitted for less than `timeSharing.interval`, kueue-priority-booster leaves the annotation unset.
+2. After that time, it sets the boost to the negative of `timeSharing.negativeBoost`.
+3. When the Workload is no longer admitted, it deletes the annotation.
 
-`effectivePriority = workload.priority + priorityBoost`
+It needs a ClusterQueue with `preemption.withinClusterQueue: LowerPriority`.
+Only one controller should write the annotation in a cluster.
 
-where `priorityBoost` is read from `Workload` annotation
-`kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` (default `0`). For preemption policies such as
-`LowerPriority` and `LowerOrNewerEqualPriority`, Kueue uses effective priority
-(instead of raw priority) when evaluating eligibility. It does not change DRS
-calculations.
+### Security, Privacy, and RBAC
 
-### Reference controller
+kueue-controller-manager needs no new permission.
+An external controller needs `get`, `list`, `watch` and `patch` on `workloads.kueue.x-k8s.io`.
+The reference controller also creates events.
+Admins give `patch` on `Workload` only to trusted controllers.
 
-Provide a reference external controller that:
+### Test Plan
 
-- Watches `Workload` objects managed by Kueue.
-- Computes priority boost using a simple, policy-neutral heuristic.
-- Writes/updates `metadata.annotations["kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost"]` on
-  the `Workload`.
+#### Unit tests
 
-Reference algorithm (example):
+- The effective priority is base priority plus boost, an absent annotation is 0, and an invalid value is rejected.
+- Queue order, preemption eligibility and candidate order use the effective priority.
+- A boost change is detected for each source of the base priority.
+- The reference controller computes the boost for representative Workload states and writes it idempotently.
 
-- Start with base boost `0`.
-- Add a bounded increment for each prior eviction with reason `Preempted`
-  observed in the Workload's `schedulingStats`. To avoid preemption flapping,
-  use a threshold-based approach: increase the boost only after every N
-  preemptions (e.g. every 2nd preemption), so that in the worst case a single
-  extra preemption can occur but the cycle does not recur. Note: this approach
-  bounds the frequency of flapping but does not eliminate it entirely.
-- Optionally add a bounded phase-based increment (for example, running
-  workloads can receive a higher boost than initializing workloads).
-- The controller can combine multiple factors (preemption count, job phase,
-  checkpoint status, crash count) into a single boost value using configurable
-  weights.
+#### Integration tests
 
-The reference implementation is intentionally simple and demonstrates how
-platform teams can plug in richer, organization-specific logic.
+- Two Workloads with the same base priority and different boosts: the preemption order follows the boost.
+- A boost that crosses a priority class boundary changes the queue order and protects the Workload under `LowerPriority`.
+- `LowerOrNewerEqualPriority`: two Workloads with different base priorities and the same effective priority count as equal.
+- `borrowWithinCohort.maxPriorityThreshold`: a boost moves a Workload across the threshold.
+- A boost change on an admitted Workload makes a pending Workload preempt it, for a WorkloadPriorityClass,
+  a Kubernetes PriorityClass and no class.
+- A Workload that stores an invalid value from a time when the feature gate was off can still be admitted and deleted.
+- The replacement slice of an elastic job keeps the boost.
+- MultiKueue: the remote copy gets the boost at creation, and the manager still replaces a remote copy that is out of sync
+  when the boosts differ.
+- With the `PriorityBoost` feature gate off, Kueue ignores the annotation and accepts any value.
+- The reference controller changes the annotation and the preemption follows.
 
-### Observability
+#### e2e tests
 
-No new scheduler metrics are required initially.
+None.
+The feature does not depend on Pods, the kubelet or kube-controller-manager, so integration tests cover it.
 
-- When a Workload create or update is rejected due to an invalid
-  `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` value, the admission response (or status)
-  should indicate the reason; optional low-verbosity log.
-- When a preemption happens, Kueue should log the effective priority (base +
-  boost) of both the preemptor and the preemptees.
-- The reference controller should log annotation updates and expose
-  reconciliation errors.
+### Graduation Criteria
 
-### Security, Privacy, and RBAC
+#### Alpha
 
-Scheduler side: no additional RBAC is required beyond existing permissions for
-reading `Workload` objects.
+- The feature is behind the `PriorityBoost` feature gate, off by default.
+- Kueue uses the effective priority for queue order, preemption eligibility and candidate order.
+- The annotation is honored on the Workload only.
+- The reference controller is available.
 
-RBAC on the `Workload` resource should be configured so that only trusted
-controllers can patch it.
+#### Beta
 
-Reference controller requires permissions to:
+- The feature gate is on by default.
+- A boost change on an admitted Workload re-evaluates pending Workloads for every source of the base priority.
+- A stored invalid value does not block updates of the Workload.
+- The replacement slice of an elastic job keeps the boost.
+- A boost that differs between the manager's Workload and a MultiKueue remote copy does not stop the manager from replacing
+  a remote copy that is out of sync.
+- The integration tests in the Test Plan are in place.
+- The docs have a concept page for the boost.
+  It states the cohort-wide effect, how to scope it with Configurable Preemptions, and what an external controller
+  must do for derived Workloads.
+  The satellite-projects page describes the single negative step of the reference controller.
 
-- `workloads.kueue.x-k8s.io`:
-  - `get`, `list`, `watch`
-- `workloads.kueue.x-k8s.io`:
-  - `patch`
+#### GA
 
-Cluster administrators should ensure that only trusted controllers have `patch`
-access to `Workload` objects to prevent abuse of the `priority-boost`
-annotation.
+- Review the annotation again as an API field, with user feedback.

 ## Summary
 
-Introduce an optional `Workload` annotation `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost`
-that allows external controllers to boost (or reduce) a workload's effective
-priority. Kueue computes the **effective priority** as:
+This KEP adds an optional `Workload` annotation, `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost`,
+so that an external controller can raise or lower the priority that Kueue uses for a Workload.
+Kueue adds the boost to the Workload's priority and uses the result wherever it compares Workload priorities.
+The Pod priority does not change.
 
-`effectivePriority = workload.priority + priorityBoost`
-
-and uses it wherever workload priority is currently used within a ClusterQueue,
-including scheduling order, preemption candidate ordering, and preemption
-eligibility evaluation.
+## Motivation
 
-This enables platform teams to dynamically adjust workload priority based on
-runtime signals (e.g. checkpoint status, execution phase, number of prior
-preemptions), without hardcoding cost policies into Kueue and without requiring
-a proliferation of WorkloadPriority classes.
+The cost of preempting a Workload is not uniform.
+Some Workloads are cheap to interrupt, for example while they initialize.
+Others are expensive, for example in the middle of an epoch with no checkpoint.
+Currently Kueue decides preemption from the ClusterQueue, the fairness configuration, the Workload priority and recency.
+Without the boost, it cannot use a runtime signal from an external system.
 
-## Motivation
+Currently the priority of a Workload changes only through its priority class.
+Platform teams need to adjust it while the Workload runs:
 
-The "cost" of preempting a workload is not uniform. Some workloads are cheap to
-interrupt (e.g. still initializing), while others are expensive (e.g. mid-epoch
-without checkpoint). Kueue today makes preemption decisions primarily based on
-cluster queue, fairness configuration, workload priority, and recency; it has no
-mechanism to incorporate runtime cost signals from external systems.
-
-Existing priority classes are static: a workload receives a priority at creation
-time and keeps it for its lifetime. In practice, platform teams need a way to
-adjust effective priority dynamically:
-
-- **Visibility**: an explicit annotation makes it clear to both users and
-  external controllers that the priority was boosted, avoiding repeated or
-  conflicting adjustments.
-- **Fine-grained priority without class proliferation**: with a numeric boost,
-  controllers can express priorities like "150" or "156" without creating a zoo
-  of WorkloadPriority classes for every combination of base priority and runtime
-  state.
-- **Controller convenience**: the annotation lives on the `Workload` object, so
-  the external controller only needs to operate on a single Kind rather than
-  every schedulable object type (Job, JobSet, Deployment, etc.).
-
-An important use case is preventing starvation of low-priority workloads: if a
-low-priority workload has been preempted too many times and has never completed,
-an external controller can boost it above medium-priority workloads so it
-eventually makes progress.
+- **Visibility**: an annotation shows users and controllers that the priority was adjusted, and by how much.
+- **Fine-grained priority without many classes**: a numeric boost can express a priority such as 150 or 156
+  without a WorkloadPriorityClass for every combination of base priority and runtime state.
+- **One object kind**: the annotation is on the `Workload`, so the external controller handles one kind,
+  not every job kind.
+- **A supported way to change priority at runtime**: some users edit `spec.priority` by hand
+  ([#14698](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/14698)).
+  Kueue can overwrite such an edit, for example when kueue-controller-manager restarts.
 
 ### Goals
 
-- Add a new optional `Workload` annotation (`kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost`)
-  which allows external controllers to boost priority for some workloads.
-- Define effective priority as `workload.priority + priorityBoost` and use it
-  for workload ordering within a ClusterQueue (scheduling and preemption).
-- Provide a reference external controller that demonstrates how to compute and
-  set `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` on `Workload`.
+- Add an optional `Workload` annotation that lets an external controller adjust the priority Kueue uses.
+- Use the adjusted priority in every place where Kueue compares Workload priorities.
+- Provide a reference external controller that sets the annotation.
 
 ### Non-Goals
 
-- Define a universal "correct" cost or boost model inside Kueue.
-- Impact DRS (Dominant Resource Share) calculations; the boost only affects
-  priority-based ordering within a ClusterQueue.
-- Define anti-flapping policies inside Kueue; it is the responsibility of the
-  external controller to avoid pathological update patterns.
-- Provide guarantees about graceful termination or checkpointing.
-- Expose Job-level APIs (annotations) for priority boost; the mechanism stays
-  Workload-only so that batch users cannot set boost on their Jobs.
+- Define a cost or boost model inside Kueue.
+- Change how Kueue computes the Dominant Resource Share (DRS).
+- Limit the effect of a boost to one ClusterQueue.
+  [Configurable Preemptions](../13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md) can scope the effect on preemption.
+- Prevent repeated preemption between Workloads inside Kueue.
+  The external controller must avoid it.
+- Guarantee graceful termination or checkpointing.
+- Let batch users set the boost on their Jobs.
+  The annotation is honored on the `Workload` only.
 
 ## Proposal
 
-Add an optional `Workload` annotation `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` that
-external controllers can set to dynamically adjust a workload's effective
-priority. Kueue incorporates this signal into both scheduling order and
-preemption candidate ordering within a ClusterQueue.
+An external controller writes one signed integer to the `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` annotation of a `Workload`.
+Kueue adds it to the Workload's priority.
+Kueue uses the sum for queue order, for preemption eligibility and for the order of preemption candidates.
+Kueue never computes or changes the annotation.
+
+### Terminology
+
+- **Base priority**: the value of the Workload's `spec.priority`.
+- **Boost**: the integer in the `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` annotation. It is 0 when the annotation is absent.
+- **Effective priority**: base priority plus boost.
+- **External controller**: a controller outside kueue-controller-manager that writes the annotation.
+- **Derived Workload**: a Workload that Kueue creates from another Workload, or for the same job as another Workload:
+  a replacement slice of an elastic job, a variant under Concurrent Admission, or a remote copy under MultiKueue.
 
 ### User Stories
 
-- As a cluster administrator, I want Kueue to preferentially preempt workloads
-  that are cheaper to interrupt, even when multiple candidates share the same
-  WorkloadPriority class.
-- As a platform team, I want to implement organization-specific logic (e.g.
-  checkpoint-aware, phase-aware, starvation-prevention) that dynamically adjusts
-  workload priority and have Kueue respect it without modifying the scheduler.
-- As a platform team, I want to boost a low-priority workload that has been
-  preempted too many times so that it can cross priority class boundaries and
-  eventually make progress.
+#### Story 1: equal-priority Workloads wait behind a long-running Workload
 
-### Effective priority
+Two Workloads have the same priority and each needs the whole quota of a ClusterQueue.
+The second one waits until the first one finishes.
+The platform team wants them to take turns.
 
-Wherever Kueue currently uses workload priority for ordering within a
-ClusterQueue, it uses instead:
+An external controller lowers the effective priority of the running Workload after it has run for one hour:
 
-`effectivePriority = workload.priority + priorityBoost`
+```yaml
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Workload
+metadata:
+  name: train-a
+  annotations:
+    kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost: "-100"
+```
 
-where `priorityBoost` is read from the `Workload` annotation
-`kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` (default `0`).
+The waiting Workload now has a higher effective priority and preempts the running one.
+This story needs a ClusterQueue with `preemption.withinClusterQueue: LowerPriority`, which is not the default.
```

  This update targets Beta, where the gate is on by default, so the story needs no gate setting. The kep-writing rules also keep feature gates out of user stories.

 ## Summary
 
-Introduce an optional `Workload` annotation `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost`
-that allows external controllers to boost (or reduce) a workload's effective
-priority. Kueue computes the **effective priority** as:
+This KEP adds an optional `Workload` annotation, `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost`,
+so that an external controller can raise or lower the priority that Kueue uses for a Workload.
+Kueue adds the boost to the Workload's priority and uses the result wherever it compares Workload priorities.
+The Pod priority does not change.
 
-`effectivePriority = workload.priority + priorityBoost`
-
-and uses it wherever workload priority is currently used within a ClusterQueue,
-including scheduling order, preemption candidate ordering, and preemption
-eligibility evaluation.
+## Motivation
 
-This enables platform teams to dynamically adjust workload priority based on
-runtime signals (e.g. checkpoint status, execution phase, number of prior
-preemptions), without hardcoding cost policies into Kueue and without requiring
-a proliferation of WorkloadPriority classes.
+The cost of preempting a Workload is not uniform.
+Some Workloads are cheap to interrupt, for example while they initialize.
+Others are expensive, for example in the middle of an epoch with no checkpoint.
+Currently Kueue decides preemption from the ClusterQueue, the fairness configuration, the Workload priority and recency.
+Without the boost, it cannot use a runtime signal from an external system.
 
-## Motivation
+Currently the priority of a Workload changes only through its priority class.
+Platform teams need to adjust it while the Workload runs:
 
-The "cost" of preempting a workload is not uniform. Some workloads are cheap to
-interrupt (e.g. still initializing), while others are expensive (e.g. mid-epoch
-without checkpoint). Kueue today makes preemption decisions primarily based on
-cluster queue, fairness configuration, workload priority, and recency; it has no
-mechanism to incorporate runtime cost signals from external systems.
-
-Existing priority classes are static: a workload receives a priority at creation
-time and keeps it for its lifetime. In practice, platform teams need a way to
-adjust effective priority dynamically:
-
-- **Visibility**: an explicit annotation makes it clear to both users and
-  external controllers that the priority was boosted, avoiding repeated or
-  conflicting adjustments.
-- **Fine-grained priority without class proliferation**: with a numeric boost,
-  controllers can express priorities like "150" or "156" without creating a zoo
-  of WorkloadPriority classes for every combination of base priority and runtime
-  state.
-- **Controller convenience**: the annotation lives on the `Workload` object, so
-  the external controller only needs to operate on a single Kind rather than
-  every schedulable object type (Job, JobSet, Deployment, etc.).
-
-An important use case is preventing starvation of low-priority workloads: if a
-low-priority workload has been preempted too many times and has never completed,
-an external controller can boost it above medium-priority workloads so it
-eventually makes progress.
+- **Visibility**: an annotation shows users and controllers that the priority was adjusted, and by how much.
+- **Fine-grained priority without many classes**: a numeric boost can express a priority such as 150 or 156
+  without a WorkloadPriorityClass for every combination of base priority and runtime state.
+- **One object kind**: the annotation is on the `Workload`, so the external controller handles one kind,
+  not every job kind.
+- **A supported way to change priority at runtime**: some users edit `spec.priority` by hand
+  ([#14698](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/14698)).
+  Kueue can overwrite such an edit, for example when kueue-controller-manager restarts.
 
 ### Goals
 
-- Add a new optional `Workload` annotation (`kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost`)
-  which allows external controllers to boost priority for some workloads.
-- Define effective priority as `workload.priority + priorityBoost` and use it
-  for workload ordering within a ClusterQueue (scheduling and preemption).
-- Provide a reference external controller that demonstrates how to compute and
-  set `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` on `Workload`.

+- Use the adjusted priority in every place where Kueue compares Workload priorities.

-  external controller to avoid pathological update patterns.

-- Expose Job-level APIs (annotations) for priority boost; the mechanism stays

```diff
@@ -199,177 +220,239 @@ metadata:
   name: example-workload
   namespace: default
   annotations:
-    # Positive values increase effective priority. Unset means 0.
     kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost: "100"
 spec:
-  priorityClassName: medium
-  # ... rest of spec
+  priorityClassRef:
+    group: kueue.x-k8s.io

 ```
 
+- The value is a signed integer. A positive value raises the effective priority and a negative value lowers it.
+- An absent annotation means a boost of 0.
+- The boost applies to every source of the base priority: a WorkloadPriorityClass, a Kubernetes PriorityClass,
+  or no class.
+- The annotation is mutable at any time, also on an admitted Workload.
+- Kueue does not copy the annotation from a Job or a Pod to the Workload.
+
 ### Validation
 
-When the `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` annotation is set or updated on a
-Workload, Kueue validates that the value is a valid signed integer. Invalid or
-unparsable values cause the Workload creation or update to be rejected (see API
-changes above).
+Kueue rejects a Workload create or update when:
+
+- the value is not a base-10 signed integer that fits in 32 bits
+- the annotation is present with an empty value.
+
+Currently Kueue validates the value on every update of the Workload.
+A Workload that stored an invalid value while the feature gate was off then rejects every later update,
+including status updates, so Kueue can neither admit it nor finish its deletion.
+From Beta, Kueue validates the value on create and on an update that changes the value.
+
+Kueue does not validate the annotation when the `PriorityBoost` feature gate is off.
+
+### Handling
+
+- For an accepted value, Kueue uses the effective priority in the comparisons listed in
+  [Interaction with other features](#interaction-with-other-features).
+- When Kueue preempts a Workload, it records an event on the preempted Workload and on the preemptor.
+  Both events give the base priority, the boost and the effective priority of the two Workloads.
+- The visibility API reports the base priority of a pending Workload.
+  The position it reports follows the effective priority.
+- When the `PriorityBoost` feature gate is off, Kueue ignores the annotation and uses the base priority.
+  The annotation stays on the Workload and applies again when an admin turns the gate on.
+- If a stored value cannot be parsed, Kueue uses the base priority.
+
+### Re-evaluation after a boost change
+
+A boost change on a pending Workload makes Kueue evaluate that Workload again.
+A boost change on an admitted Workload makes Kueue evaluate the inadmissible Workloads of its ClusterQueue again.
+When the ClusterQueue is in a cohort, Kueue evaluates the inadmissible Workloads of the whole cohort tree,
+because one of them may now be able to preempt it.
+
+### Derived Workloads
+
+| Derived Workload | Boost at creation | Later change of the boost on the original |
+|---|---|---|
+| Replacement slice of an elastic job | Currently not copied. From Beta, Kueue copies it from the slice it replaces. | Does not apply. The replaced slice is finished. |
+| Variant under Concurrent Admission | Copied from the parent Workload. | Not copied. |
+| Remote copy under MultiKueue | Copied from the manager's Workload. | Not copied. |
+
+Kueue creates the replacement slice in the same cluster to continue the same job,
+and an external controller cannot set the annotation before the new slice is evaluated.
+So Kueue keeps the boost across that replacement.
+
+A variant and a remote copy are separate Workloads that can be annotated directly.
+An external controller that wants to boost a job under Concurrent Admission annotates the variants.
+Under MultiKueue the worker cluster decides preemption, so an external controller runs in each worker cluster.
+
+### Interaction with other features
+
+| Feature | What the boost changes |
+|---|---|
+| Queue order in a ClusterQueue | Workloads are ordered by effective priority, then by timestamp. |
+| Admission Fair Sharing | LocalQueue usage is compared before priority. The boost only orders Workloads whose LocalQueues have equal usage. |
+| Order of a scheduling cycle across a cohort | After Workloads that fit without borrowing, a higher effective priority goes first. This needs the `PrioritySortingWithinCohort` feature gate, which is on by default. |
+| Fair Sharing | DRS values do not change. When two ClusterQueues tie on DRS, the Workload with the higher effective priority goes first, under the same feature gate. |
+| `withinClusterQueue` and `reclaimWithinCohort` policies | `LowerPriority` compares effective priorities, and so does `LowerOrNewerEqualPriority` of `withinClusterQueue`. Two Workloads with different base priorities and the same effective priority count as equal. |
+| `borrowWithinCohort.maxPriorityThreshold` | The threshold is compared with the effective priority of the candidate. |
+| Order of preemption candidates | Candidates with a lower effective priority are preempted first, after the existing rules for evicted Workloads, other ClusterQueues and LocalQueue usage. |
+| Configurable Preemptions (Alpha) | A priority selector chooses `Boosted` (effective priority) or `Base` (base priority). In Alpha, configured candidates are added after the classical ones, and the classical rules use the effective priority. |
+| Scheduling equivalence classes | The effective priority is part of the class. Two Workloads that differ only in boost are in different classes. |
+| WorkloadPriorityClass value change | Kueue updates the base priority. The boost stays, so the effective priority moves by the same amount. |
+| Elastic jobs, Concurrent Admission, MultiKueue | See [Derived Workloads](#derived-workloads). |
+| Topology Aware Scheduling | No change. |
 
-### Scheduler changes
+### Reference controller
 
-Kueue-scheduler uses **effective priority** for all mechanics where **base
-priority** is currently used. In particular, it is used for:
+`kueue-priority-booster` is an experimental controller in the Kueue repository.
+Admins deploy it separately from kueue-controller-manager.
+It implements Story 1:
 
-1. Ordering of heads for scheduling.
-2. Selection of preemption candidates.
+1. While a Workload is admitted for less than `timeSharing.interval`, kueue-priority-booster leaves the annotation unset.
+2. After that time, it sets the boost to the negative of `timeSharing.negativeBoost`.
+3. When the Workload is no longer admitted, it deletes the annotation.
 
-`effectivePriority = workload.priority + priorityBoost`
+It needs a ClusterQueue with `preemption.withinClusterQueue: LowerPriority`.
+Only one controller should write the annotation in a cluster.
 
-where `priorityBoost` is read from `Workload` annotation
-`kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` (default `0`). For preemption policies such as
-`LowerPriority` and `LowerOrNewerEqualPriority`, Kueue uses effective priority
-(instead of raw priority) when evaluating eligibility. It does not change DRS
-calculations.
+### Security, Privacy, and RBAC
 
-### Reference controller
+kueue-controller-manager needs no new permission.
+An external controller needs `get`, `list`, `watch` and `patch` on `workloads.kueue.x-k8s.io`.
+The reference controller also creates events.
+Admins give `patch` on `Workload` only to trusted controllers.
 
-Provide a reference external controller that:
+### Test Plan
 
-- Watches `Workload` objects managed by Kueue.
-- Computes priority boost using a simple, policy-neutral heuristic.
-- Writes/updates `metadata.annotations["kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost"]` on
-  the `Workload`.
+#### Unit tests
 
-Reference algorithm (example):
+- The effective priority is base priority plus boost, an absent annotation is 0, and an invalid value is rejected.
+- Queue order, preemption eligibility and candidate order use the effective priority.
+- A boost change is detected for each source of the base priority.
+- The reference controller computes the boost for representative Workload states and writes it idempotently.
 
-- Start with base boost `0`.
-- Add a bounded increment for each prior eviction with reason `Preempted`
-  observed in the Workload's `schedulingStats`. To avoid preemption flapping,
-  use a threshold-based approach: increase the boost only after every N
-  preemptions (e.g. every 2nd preemption), so that in the worst case a single
-  extra preemption can occur but the cycle does not recur. Note: this approach
-  bounds the frequency of flapping but does not eliminate it entirely.
-- Optionally add a bounded phase-based increment (for example, running
-  workloads can receive a higher boost than initializing workloads).
-- The controller can combine multiple factors (preemption count, job phase,
-  checkpoint status, crash count) into a single boost value using configurable
-  weights.
+#### Integration tests
 
-The reference implementation is intentionally simple and demonstrates how
-platform teams can plug in richer, organization-specific logic.
+- Two Workloads with the same base priority and different boosts: the preemption order follows the boost.
+- A boost that crosses a priority class boundary changes the queue order and protects the Workload under `LowerPriority`.
+- `LowerOrNewerEqualPriority`: two Workloads with different base priorities and the same effective priority count as equal.
+- `borrowWithinCohort.maxPriorityThreshold`: a boost moves a Workload across the threshold.
+- A boost change on an admitted Workload makes a pending Workload preempt it, for a WorkloadPriorityClass,
+  a Kubernetes PriorityClass and no class.
+- A Workload that stores an invalid value from a time when the feature gate was off can still be admitted and deleted.
+- The replacement slice of an elastic job keeps the boost.
+- MultiKueue: the remote copy gets the boost at creation, and the manager still replaces a remote copy that is out of sync
+  when the boosts differ.
+- With the `PriorityBoost` feature gate off, Kueue ignores the annotation and accepts any value.
+- The reference controller changes the annotation and the preemption follows.
 
-### Observability
+#### e2e tests
 
-No new scheduler metrics are required initially.
+None.
+The feature does not depend on Pods, the kubelet or kube-controller-manager, so integration tests cover it.
 
-- When a Workload create or update is rejected due to an invalid
-  `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` value, the admission response (or status)
-  should indicate the reason; optional low-verbosity log.
-- When a preemption happens, Kueue should log the effective priority (base +
-  boost) of both the preemptor and the preemptees.
-- The reference controller should log annotation updates and expose
-  reconciliation errors.
+### Graduation Criteria
 
-### Security, Privacy, and RBAC
+#### Alpha
 
-Scheduler side: no additional RBAC is required beyond existing permissions for
-reading `Workload` objects.
+- The feature is behind the `PriorityBoost` feature gate, off by default.
+- Kueue uses the effective priority for queue order, preemption eligibility and candidate order.
+- The annotation is honored on the Workload only.
+- The reference controller is available.
 
-RBAC on the `Workload` resource should be configured so that only trusted
-controllers can patch it.
+#### Beta
 
-Reference controller requires permissions to:
+- The feature gate is on by default.
```

- **keps/7990-priority-boost/README.md:302** by [@coderabbitai[bot]](https://github.com/apps/coderabbitai) — 2026-11-18T16:24:24Z
```diff
@@ -199,177 +220,240 @@ metadata:
   name: example-workload
   namespace: default
   annotations:
-    # Positive values increase effective priority. Unset means 0.
     kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost: "100"
 spec:
-  priorityClassName: medium
-  # ... rest of spec
+  priorityClassRef:
+    group: kueue.x-k8s.io
+    kind: WorkloadPriorityClass
+    name: medium
+  priority: 200
+  # podSets omitted
 ```
 
+- The value is a signed integer. A positive value raises the effective priority and a negative value lowers it.
+- An absent annotation means a boost of 0.
+- The boost applies to every source of the base priority: a WorkloadPriorityClass, a Kubernetes PriorityClass,
+  or no class.
+- The annotation is mutable at any time, also on an admitted Workload.
+- Kueue does not copy the annotation from a Job or a Pod to the Workload.
+
 ### Validation
 
-When the `kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost` annotation is set or updated on a
-Workload, Kueue validates that the value is a valid signed integer. Invalid or
-unparsable values cause the Workload creation or update to be rejected (see API
-changes above).
+Kueue rejects a Workload create or update when:
+
+- the value is not a base-10 signed integer that fits in 32 bits
+- the annotation is present with an empty value.
+
+Currently Kueue validates the value on every update of the Workload.
+A Workload that stored an invalid value while the feature gate was off then rejects every later update,
+including status updates, so Kueue can neither admit it nor finish its deletion.
+From Beta, Kueue validates the value on create and on an update that changes the value.
+
+Kueue does not validate the annotation when the `PriorityBoost` feature gate is off.
+
+### Handling
+
+- For an accepted value, Kueue uses the effective priority in the comparisons listed in
+  [Interaction with other features](#interaction-with-other-features).
+- When Kueue preempts a Workload, it records an event on the preempted Workload and on the preemptor.
+  Both events give the base priority, the boost and the effective priority of the two Workloads.
+- The visibility API reports the base priority of a pending Workload.
+  The position it reports follows the effective priority.
+- When the `PriorityBoost` feature gate is off, Kueue ignores the annotation and uses the base priority.
+  The annotation stays on the Workload and applies again when an admin turns the gate on.
+- If a stored value cannot be parsed, Kueue uses the base priority.
+
+### Re-evaluation after a boost change
+
+A boost change on a pending Workload makes Kueue evaluate that Workload again.
+A boost change on an admitted Workload makes Kueue evaluate the inadmissible Workloads of its ClusterQueue again.
+When the ClusterQueue is in a cohort, Kueue evaluates the inadmissible Workloads of the whole cohort tree,
+because one of them may now be able to preempt it.
+
+### Derived Workloads
+
+| Derived Workload | Boost at creation | Later change of the boost on the original |
+|---|---|---|
+| Replacement slice of an elastic job | Currently not copied. From Beta, Kueue copies it from the slice it replaces. | Does not apply. The replaced slice is finished. |
+| Variant under Concurrent Admission | Copied from the parent Workload. | Not copied. |
+| Remote copy under MultiKueue | Copied from the manager's Workload. | Not copied. |
+
+Kueue creates the replacement slice in the same cluster to continue the same job,
+and an external controller cannot set the annotation before the new slice is evaluated.
+So Kueue keeps the boost across that replacement.
+
+A variant and a remote copy are separate Workloads that can be annotated directly.
+An external controller that wants to boost a job under Concurrent Admission annotates the variants.
+Under MultiKueue the worker cluster decides preemption, so an external controller runs in each worker cluster.
+
+### Interaction with other features
+
+| Feature | What the boost changes |
+|---|---|
+| Queue order in a ClusterQueue | Workloads are ordered by effective priority, then by timestamp. |
+| Admission Fair Sharing | LocalQueue usage is compared before priority. The boost only orders Workloads whose LocalQueues have equal usage. |
+| Order of a scheduling cycle across a cohort | After Workloads that fit without borrowing, a higher effective priority goes first. This needs the `PrioritySortingWithinCohort` feature gate, which is on by default. |
+| Fair Sharing | DRS values do not change. When two ClusterQueues tie on DRS, the Workload with the higher effective priority goes first, under the same feature gate. |
+| `withinClusterQueue` and `reclaimWithinCohort` policies | `LowerPriority` compares effective priorities, and so does `LowerOrNewerEqualPriority` of `withinClusterQueue`. Two Workloads with different base priorities and the same effective priority count as equal. |
+| `borrowWithinCohort.maxPriorityThreshold` | The threshold is compared with the effective priority of the candidate. |
+| Order of preemption candidates | Candidates with a lower effective priority are preempted first, after the existing rules for evicted Workloads, other ClusterQueues and LocalQueue usage. |
+| Configurable Preemptions (Alpha) | A priority selector chooses `Boosted` (effective priority) or `Base` (base priority). In Alpha, configured candidates are added after the classical ones, and the classical rules use the effective priority. |
+| Scheduling equivalence classes | The effective priority is part of the class. Two Workloads that differ only in boost are in different classes. |
+| WorkloadPriorityClass value change | Kueue updates the base priority. The boost stays, so the effective priority moves by the same amount. |
```

+| Derived Workload | Boost at creation | Later change of the boost on the original |
+|---|---|---|
+| Replacement slice of an elastic job | Currently not copied. From Beta, Kueue copies it from the slice it replaces. | Does not apply. The replaced slice is finished. |
+| Variant under Concurrent Admission | Copied from the parent Workload. | Not copied. |
+| Remote copy under MultiKueue | Copied from the manager's Workload. | Not copied. |
+
+Kueue creates the replacement slice in the same cluster to continue the same job,
+and an external controller cannot set the annotation before the new slice is evaluated.
+So Kueue keeps the boost across that replacement.
+
+A variant and a remote copy are separate Workloads that can be annotated directly.
+An external controller that wants to boost a job under Concurrent Admission annotates the variants.
+Under MultiKueue the worker cluster decides preemption, so an external controller runs in each worker cluster.
+
+### Interaction with other features
+
+| Feature | What the boost changes |
+|---|---|
+| Queue order in a ClusterQueue | Workloads are ordered by effective priority, then by timestamp. |
+| Admission Fair Sharing | LocalQueue usage is compared before priority. The boost only orders Workloads whose LocalQueues have equal usage. |
+| Order of a scheduling cycle across a cohort | After Workloads that fit without borrowing, a higher effective priority goes first. This needs the `PrioritySortingWithinCohort` feature gate, which is on by default. |
+| Fair Sharing | DRS values do not change. When two ClusterQueues tie on DRS, the Workload with the higher effective priority goes first, under the same feature gate. |
+| `withinClusterQueue` and `reclaimWithinCohort` policies | `LowerPriority` compares effective priorities, and so does `LowerOrNewerEqualPriority` of `withinClusterQueue`. Two Workloads with different base priorities and the same effective priority count as equal. |
+| `borrowWithinCohort.maxPriorityThreshold` | The threshold is compared with the effective priority of the candidate. |
+| Order of preemption candidates | Candidates with a lower effective priority are preempted first, after the existing rules for evicted Workloads, other ClusterQueues and LocalQueue usage. |
+| Configurable Preemptions (Alpha) | A priority selector chooses `Boosted` (effective priority) or `Base` (base priority). In Alpha, configured candidates are added after the classical ones, and the classical rules use the effective priority. |
+| Scheduling equivalence classes | The effective priority is part of the class. Two Workloads that differ only in boost are in different classes. |
+| WorkloadPriorityClass value change | Kueue updates the base priority. The boost stays, so the effective priority moves by the same amount. |
+| Elastic jobs, Concurrent Admission, MultiKueue | See [Derived Workloads](#derived-workloads). |
+| Topology Aware Scheduling | No change. |

-where `priorityBoost` is read from `Workload` annotation

-- Watches `Workload` objects managed by Kueue.
-- Computes priority boost using a simple, policy-neutral heuristic.
-- Writes/updates `metadata.annotations["kueue.x-k8s.io/priority-boost"]` on
-  the `Workload`.
+#### Unit tests
 
-Reference algorithm (example):
+- The effective priority is base priority plus boost, an absent annotation is 0, and an invalid value is rejected.
+- Queue order, preemption eligibility and candidate order use the effective priority.
+- A boost change is detected for each source of the base priority.
+- The reference controller computes the boost for representative Workload states and writes it idempotently.
 
-- Start with base boost `0`.
-- Add a bounded increment for each prior eviction with reason `Preempted`
-  observed in the Workload's `schedulingStats`. To avoid preemption flapping,
-  use a threshold-based approach: increase the boost only after every N
-  preemptions (e.g. every 2nd preemption), so that in the worst case a single
-  extra preemption can occur but the cycle does not recur. Note: this approach
-  bounds the frequency of flapping but does not eliminate it entirely.
-- Optionally add a bounded phase-based increment (for example, running
-  workloads can receive a higher boost than initializing workloads).
-- The controller can combine multiple factors (preemption count, job phase,
-  checkpoint status, crash count) into a single boost value using configurable
-  weights.
+#### Integration tests
 
-The reference implementation is intentionally simple and demonstrates how
-platform teams can plug in richer, organization-specific logic.
+- Two Workloads with the same base priority and different boosts: the preemption order follows the boost.
+- A boost that crosses a priority class boundary changes the queue order and protects the Workload under `LowerPriority`.
+- `LowerOrNewerEqualPriority`: two Workloads with different base priorities and the same effective priority count as equal.
+- `borrowWithinCohort.maxPriorityThreshold`: a boost moves a Workload across the threshold.
+- A boost change on an admitted Workload makes a pending Workload preempt it, for a WorkloadPriorityClass,
+  a Kubernetes PriorityClass and no class.
+- A Workload that stores an invalid value from a time when the feature gate was off can still be admitted and deleted.
+- The replacement slice of an elastic job keeps the boost.
+- MultiKueue: the remote copy gets the boost at creation, and the manager still replaces a remote copy that is out of sync
+  when the boosts differ.
+- With the `PriorityBoost` feature gate off, Kueue ignores the annotation and accepts any value.
+- The reference controller changes the annotation and the preemption follows.

+## Implementation History
 
-#### Unit tests
+- 2026-01-12: Initial KEP drafted as a "preemption cost" design.

+## Drawbacks
 
-### Graduation Criteria
+- One more signal makes scheduling and preemption harder to reason about when it is set incorrectly.
+- A boost can cross priority class boundaries, which surprises users who expect strict separation of classes.
+- A bug in an external controller can cause repeated preemption or starvation.

-  or GA, to avoid exposing boost to batch users.

-  preemption candidate ordering, not scheduling) is needed alongside
-  `priority-boost`.

-  different base priorities but the same effective priority after boost, confirm
-  the policy treats them as equal-priority.
-- Evaluate whether a `maxPriorityBoost` cap on ClusterQueue is needed as a
-  safety valve against runaway controllers.
+### A preemption-cost annotation that only orders preemption candidates
 
-Interaction of DRS (Dominant Resource Share) with priority-boost is out of scope
-for this KEP; it may be re-evaluated for GA or handled in a separate KEP/Issue.
+The first design in this KEP proposed a `kueue.x-k8s.io/preemption-cost` annotation that changed only the order of candidates.
+It was rejected for two reasons.
+A preempted mid-priority Workload could at once preempt the low-priority Workload with the high cost,
+because eligibility still used the static priorities.
+Starvation prevention needs to cross priority class boundaries, which candidate ordering cannot do.
 
-Beta -> GA:
+The Alpha KEP left open whether to add such a mechanism next to the boost.
+It is not added.
+A Configurable Preemptions rule with priority mode `Base` gives preemption that ignores the boost.
 
-- After Beta validation, graduate to GA per standard criteria. The Alpha -> Beta
-  items above are the main gates; DRS interaction may be re-evaluated at GA.

-## Implementation History
+Rejected.
+It needs a WorkloadPriorityClass for every combination of base priority and runtime state,
+it does not show that a priority was adjusted,

-- 2026-02-20: KEP rewritten to "priority boost" approach based on community
-  feedback in #7990 and PR #8551.
+### A cost or boost policy inside Kueue
 
-## Drawbacks
+Rejected, to keep Kueue neutral about policy and to let external controllers express organization-specific logic.
 
-- Adds another signal that can make scheduling and preemption behavior more
-  complex to reason about if set incorrectly.
-- Crossing priority class boundaries via boost may surprise users who expect
-  strict priority class separation.
-- External controller bugs can cause preemption flapping or starvation; Kueue
-  itself does not enforce anti-flapping policies.
+### A boost field in the Workload spec with a strategy
 
-## Alternatives
+[#13414](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/13414) proposed `spec.priorityBoost` with a field that says when the boost applies,
+for example only when the preempted Workloads are in the same ClusterQueue.
+It was closed in favor of Configurable Preemptions, which expresses the same scoping per ClusterQueue.
